1	/**
2	 * Integration tests for the AgentRuntimeManager retention/reaper paths
3	 * introduced to fix the memory-pressure crash:
4	 *
5	 *   - child exit → grace-period reap removes the record from this.agents
6	 *   - idle agents older than OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS get stopAgent'd
7	 *     by the periodic sweep
8	 *   - MemoryTracer emits spawn + exit records for every started child
9	 *
10	 * Drives the manager with a tiny stub ACP CLI (Node script) so we don't depend
11	 * on any real agent binary.
12	 */
13	import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
14	import { mkdtempSync, readFileSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "fs";
15	import { tmpdir } from "os";
16	import { join } from "path";
17	import { AgentRuntimeManager } from "@/server/agent-runtime/manager";
18	import { acquireWorkerSpawnResources } from "@/server/agent-runtime/resource-admission";
19	import { __resetNamedEventsForTests, getNamedEventsSince } from "@/server/events/named-events";
20	
21	const tempDirs: string[] = [];
22	
23	function createTempDir(prefix: string) {
24	  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), prefix));
25	  tempDirs.push(dir);
26	  return dir;
27	}
28	
29	function createExecutable(dir: string, name: string, contents: string) {
30	  const filePath = join(dir, name);
31	  writeFileSync(filePath, contents, { mode: 0o755 });
32	  return filePath;
33	}
34	
35	// Minimal ACP CLI: responds to initialize + session/new, then stays alive
36	// reading from stdin until the parent kills it. Enough for the manager to
37	// register the agent in its `agents` map.
38	const minimalAcpScript = `#!/usr/bin/env node
39	process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');
40	let buffer = '';
41	
42	function write(message) {
43	  process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(message) + '\\n');
44	}
45	
46	process.stdin.on('data', (chunk) => {
47	  buffer += chunk;
48	  const lines = buffer.split(/\\r?\\n/g);
49	  buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
50	  for (const line of lines) {
51	    if (!line.trim()) continue;
52	    const message = JSON.parse(line);
53	    if (message.method === 'initialize') {
54	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id: message.id, result: { protocolVersion: 1 } });
55	    } else if (message.method === 'session/new') {
56	      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id: message.id, result: { sessionId: 'session-1' } });
57	    }
58	  }
59	});
60	
61	// Keep alive until parent signals exit.
62	process.stdin.on('end', () => process.exit(0));
63	`;
64	
65	async function sleep(ms: number) {
66	  await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
67	}
68	
69	async function startAgent(manager: AgentRuntimeManager, dir: string, name: string) {
70	  const command = createExecutable(dir, `acp-${name}.js`, minimalAcpScript);
71	  return manager.startAgent({
72	    type: "gemini",
73	    name,
74	    cwd: dir,
75	    command,
76	    args: [],
77	  });
78	}
79	
80	afterEach(() => {
81	  __resetNamedEventsForTests();
82	  for (const dir of tempDirs.splice(0)) {
83	    rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
84	  }
85	});

87	describe("AgentRuntimeManager exit-grace reaper", () => {
88	  it("removes the agent record from this.agents after the grace period when the child exits", async () => {
89	    const dir = createTempDir("omni-reaper-exit-");
90	    const manager = new AgentRuntimeManager({
91	      env: {
92	        ...process.env,
93	        OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_EXIT_GRACE_MS: "80",
94	        OMNIHARNESS_RUNTIME_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS: "10000",
95	        OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS: "999999",
96	        OMNIHARNESS_MEMORY_TRACE: "0",
97	      } as Record<string, string>,
98	    });
99	    try {
100	      const status = await startAgent(manager, dir, "reap-exit");
101	      expect(status.name).toBe("reap-exit");
102	      expect(manager.agents.has("reap-exit")).toBe(true);
103	
104	      // Force the child to exit. The exit handler should mark state stopped
105	      // and schedule the grace-period reap.
106	      const record = manager.agents.get("reap-exit")!;
107	      record.child.kill("SIGTERM");
108	
109	      // Before grace window elapses, the record should still be present
110	      // (tombstone for observers).
111	      await sleep(20);
112	      expect(manager.agents.has("reap-exit")).toBe(true);
113	      expect(["stopped", "error"]).toContain(manager.agents.get("reap-exit")!.state);
114	
115	      // After grace window, the record must be gone.
116	      await sleep(200);
117	      expect(manager.agents.has("reap-exit")).toBe(false);
118	    } finally {
119	      manager.shutdownPools();
120	    }
121	  });
122	
123	  it("does not reap if the record was restarted to a non-terminal state during the grace window", async () => {
124	    const dir = createTempDir("omni-reaper-restart-");
125	    const manager = new AgentRuntimeManager({

127	        ...process.env,

129	        OMNIHARNESS_RUNTIME_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS: "10000",
130	        OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS: "999999",
131	        OMNIHARNESS_MEMORY_TRACE: "0",
132	      } as Record<string, string>,
133	    });
134	    try {
135	      await startAgent(manager, dir, "reap-restart");
136	      const record = manager.agents.get("reap-restart")!;
137	      record.child.kill("SIGTERM");
138	      await sleep(20);
139	      expect(["stopped", "error"]).toContain(manager.agents.get("reap-restart")!.state);
140	
141	      // Simulate the record being revived (e.g. restart under the same name).
142	      manager.agents.get("reap-restart")!.state = "idle";
143	
144	      await sleep(200);
145	      // Reap timer should have checked state and bailed because the record is
146	      // no longer in a terminal state.
147	      expect(manager.agents.has("reap-restart")).toBe(true);
148	      expect(manager.agents.get("reap-restart")!.state).toBe("idle");
149	    } finally {

151	      manager.agents.delete("reap-restart");
152	      manager.shutdownPools();
153	    }
154	  });
155	});

304	      } as Record<string, string>,

308	      // Let the spawn event hit disk (appendFile is async).

313	      await sleep(100);
314	
315	      const content = readFileSync(tracePath, "utf8");
316	      const lines = content.split("\n").filter(Boolean).map((line) => JSON.parse(line));
317	      const kinds = new Set(lines.map((line) => line.kind));
318	      expect(kinds.has("boot")).toBe(true);
319	      expect(kinds.has("spawn")).toBe(true);

322	      const spawn = lines.find((line) => line.kind === "spawn");
323	      expect(spawn.type).toBe("gemini");
324	      expect(spawn.purpose).toBe("run");
325	      expect(typeof spawn.pid).toBe("number");
326	
327	      const exit = lines.find((line) => line.kind === "exit");
328	      expect(exit.pid).toBe(spawn.pid);
329	      expect(typeof exit.livedMs).toBe("number");
330	    } finally {
331	      manager.shutdownPools();
332	    }
333	  });
334	});

          appendOutputEntry(record, {
            type: "agent_content",
            text: item.content.type,
            toolCallId: update.toolCallId,
            raw: { content: item.content, toolCallId: update.toolCallId },
          });
        } else if (item.type === "terminal") {
          appendOutputEntry(record, {
            type: "agent_content",
            text: "",
            toolCallId: update.toolCallId,
            status: update.status ?? undefined,
            raw: { content: { type: "terminal", terminalId: item.terminalId }, toolCallId: update.toolCallId },
          });
        }
      }

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:27:26.000Z

Since named-events.ts is already being edited by another session, I should be careful to just add my change additively rather than clobbering anything—adding a "pending_agent_wake" reason member is small and safe. I'm planning to add a pendingWakeUntil field to AgentRecord and handle it in the runtime-client's sessionUpdate logic for tool_call_update events.

For deadlines, I'll compute them based on the tool type: persistent Monitor calls get treated as having no deadline (Infinity) since the agent explicitly asked for that, while ScheduleWakeup uses its scheduledFor timestamp. I'm adding a grace period of about 2 minutes to account for the time it takes for the wake signal to propagate through OmniHarness, and I've decided to keep the scope limited to just Monitor and ScheduleWakeup rather than also covering background Bash tasks, since those don't have a clear deadline semantics.

I'm also weighing whether background Agent tasks (sub-agents) need protection too, since killing the parent would kill its children — but without a clean way to map a wake notification back to a specific task deadline, I'll leave that out of scope for now and just note it. For isStaleIdleAgent, I'll have it return false while there's a pending wake, and emit a single "pending_agent_wake" skip event per idle period by reusing the existing goal-protection tracking map.

Good, a 2-minute grace period handles the race where a wake arrives right at the deadline since updatedAt resets and the idle clock restarts. I need to locate how the raw ACP update exposes toolName and toolResponse, and plan a small helper module that reads the armed wake deadline from a session update — extracting fields like taskId, timeoutMs, persistent for monitor calls, and scheduledFor/clampedDelaySeconds for scheduled wakeups.

I should also account for ScheduleWakeup calls with stop:true and no scheduledFor, which should just be ignored. Separately, the 05:30 restart issue is a bigger, distinct fix worth describing on its own, and it's worth flagging that the Monitor tool's default 30-minute timeout nearly matches the idle timeout, making this race condition almost inevitable. Before finalizing, I want to double check that manager.ts, runtime-client.ts, and types.ts are clean against git diff, and quickly review named-events.ts since it's dirty to avoid any conflicts.

@@ -306,6 +306,108 @@ export async function handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker(args: {
   return { kind: "accepted", revision: result.snapshot.revision };
 }
 
+const STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP_NOTICE = /\bA hook blocked the turn from ending \d+ consecutive times\b/i;
+
+/**
+ * Claude Code's notice that a Stop hook blocked the turn too many times in a
+ * row and was overridden. The adapter delivers it as an agent message chunk
+ * because this client does not take ACP notices.
+ */
+export function isStopHookBlockCapNotice(update: unknown) {
+  if (!isGoalRecord(update) || update.sessionUpdate !== "agent_message_chunk") return false;
+  const content = update.content;
+  return isGoalRecord(content) && typeof content.text === "string" && STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP_NOTICE.test(content.text);
+}
+
+export type GoalStopHookCapResult =
+  | { kind: "ignored"; reason: string }
+  | { kind: "parked"; revision: number; dispatch: string };
+
+/**
+ * Park the worker's goal on the user after Claude overrode its `/goal` Stop hook.
+ *
+ * Claude's `/goal` is a Stop hook that blocks every turn from ending until an
+ * evaluator says the condition holds. When what is left needs the user (a
+ * device, a recording, a decision), the agent can only answer "still blocked"
+ * until Claude Code gives up after its block cap. The goal stays armed, so every
+ * later turn (each user message, each background task notification) replayed
+ * the same run of "still blocked" replies ending in the cap warning. The cap is
+ * the signal that the agent has stopped pursuing the goal, so the goal moves to
+ * `waiting_user` and is disarmed in the agent until the user resumes it.
+ */
+export async function parkGoalAfterStopHookBlockCap(args: {
+  workerId: string;
+  sessionId: string | null | undefined;
+  dispatch?: (
+    snapshot: import("@/shared/goal-plan").GoalSnapshot,
+    action: import("@/shared/goal-plan").GoalMutationAction,
+  ) => Promise<{ kind: string; reason?: string }>;
+}): Promise<GoalStopHookCapResult> {
+  const { emitNamedEvent } = await import("@/server/events/named-events");
+  const runId = await resolveRunIdForGoalWorker(args.workerId);
+  const skip = (reason: string, goalId: string | null = null): GoalStopHookCapResult => {
+    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "goal.park_skipped", runId, goalId, workerId: args.workerId, reason });
+    return { kind: "ignored", reason };
+  };
+  if (!runId) return skip("worker_not_persisted");
+  const { goalControl } = await import("@/server/runs/goal-control");
+  const current = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
+  if (!current || !current.visible || current.status === "cleared") return skip("goal_absent");
+  if (current.status !== "pursuing") return skip(`goal_${current.status}`, current.goalId);
+  const sessionId = typeof args.sessionId === "string" ? args.sessionId.trim() : "";
+  if (current.workerId !== args.workerId || current.acpSessionId !== sessionId) {
+    return skip("stale_lease", current.goalId);
+  }
+  const parked = await goalControl.markWaitingForUser(current);
+  if (!parked.ok) return skip(parked.code, current.goalId);
+  emitNamedEvent({
+    kind: "goal.parked_on_user",
+    runId,
+    goalId: parked.snapshot.goalId,
+    workerId: args.workerId,
+    reason: "stop_hook_block_cap",
+    revision: parked.snapshot.revision,
+  });
+
+  const [{ goalControlDispatchCoordinator, recoveryAction }, { goalOutboxDispatcher }] = await Promise.all([
+    import("@/server/runs/goal-control-dispatch"),
+    import("@/server/runs/goal-outbox"),
+  ]);
+  const action = recoveryAction(parked.snapshot);
+  let dispatch: string;
+  try {
+    // The agent is usually still ending the capped turn, so this mostly parks
+    // as a deferred control that the turn-settled sync replays.
+    const outcome = args.dispatch
+      ? await args.dispatch(parked.snapshot, action)
+      : await goalControlDispatchCoordinator.dispatch(parked.snapshot, action);
+    dispatch = outcome.kind;
+    if (outcome.kind !== "dispatched" && outcome.kind !== "already_applied" && outcome.kind !== "deferred") {
+      emitNamedEvent({
+        kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
+        runId,
+        goalId: parked.snapshot.goalId,
+        workerId: args.workerId,
+        reason: outcome.reason ?? outcome.kind,
+      });
+    }
+  } catch (error) {
+    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
+    dispatch = "failed";
+    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed", runId, goalId: parked.snapshot.goalId, workerId: args.workerId, reason });
+    emitNamedEvent({
+      kind: "error.surfaced",
+      code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
+      message: reason,
+      surface: "log",
+      runId,
+      workerId: args.workerId,
+    });
+  }
+  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
+  return { kind: "parked", revision: parked.snapshot.revision, dispatch };
+}
+
 export async function initializeWorkerGoalSession(
   workerId: string,
   sessionId: string,
@@ -377,7 +479,7 @@ export async function initializeWorkerGoalSession(
   }

  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/acp/armed-wakes.ts",
  "content": "import type { AgentRecord } from \"../types\";\n\n/**\n * How long past an armed wake's deadline the agent stays protected. The wake\n * starts a turn inside the agent, and only that turn's first output reaches the\n * runtime and refreshes `updatedAt`; until then the agent still reads as idle.\n */\nexport const ARMED_WAKE_GRACE_MS = 2 * 60_000;\n\nfunction asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {\n  return value && typeof value === \"object\" && !Array.isArray(value)\n    ? value as Record<string, unknown>\n    : null;\n}\n\nfunction positiveNumber(value: unknown) {\n  return typeof value === \"number\" && Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0 ? value : null;\n}\n\n/**\n * When the agent will next wake itself, from a completed Claude Code `Monitor`\n * or `ScheduleWakeup` tool call. Infinity for a persistent monitor, which can\n * fire until the session ends.\n */\nexport function readArmedWakeDeadline(update: unknown, now: number): number | null {\n  const record = asRecord(update);\n  if (!record || record.sessionUpdate !== \"tool_call_update\") return null;\n  const claudeCode = asRecord(asRecord(record._meta)?.claudeCode);\n  const response = asRecord(claudeCode?.toolResponse);\n  if (!response) return null;\n  if (claudeCode?.toolName === \"Monitor\") {\n    if (response.persistent === true) return Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY;\n    const timeoutMs = positiveNumber(response.timeoutMs);\n    return timeoutMs === null ? null : now + timeoutMs;\n  }\n  if (claudeCode?.toolName === \"ScheduleWakeup\") {\n    return positiveNumber(response.scheduledFor);\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Remember a wake the agent armed for itself. Such an agent ends its turn and\n * waits for the wake to start the next one, so the idle reaper must leave it\n * alone until then. Reaping it killed the agent and the monitors watching its\n * background work, and nothing started it again.\n */\nexport function recordArmedWake(record: Pick<AgentRecord, \"armedWakeUntil\">, update: unknown, now = Date.now()) {\n  const deadline = readArmedWakeDeadline(update, now);\n  if (deadline === null) return;\n  const protectedUntil = deadline + ARMED_WAKE_GRACE_MS;\n  if ((record.armedWakeUntil ?? 0) < protectedUntil) record.armedWakeUntil = protectedUntil;\n}\n\nexport function hasArmedWake(record: Pick<AgentRecord, \"armedWakeUntil\">, now: number) {\n  return typeof record.armedWakeUntil === \"number\" && now < record.armedWakeUntil;\n}\n"
}

Reaping it killed the agent and its monitors, so the\n   * notification had nothing to wake and the goal stalled until the user\n   * noticed. When the lookup fails nothing is reaped: a live agent costs memory,\n   * a reaped one costs the user's work.\n   */\n  private async reapStaleIdleAgents(names: string[]) {\n    if (this.idleReapInFlight) return;\n    this.idleReapInFlight = true;\n    try {\n      let goalHolders: Set<string>;\n      try {\n        goalHolders = await this.findGoalHoldingAgents(names);\n      } catch (error) {\n        process.stderr.write(`[runtime-sweep] goal lookup failed; skipping idle reap: ${describeUnknownError(error)}\\n`);\n        return;\n      }\n      const now = Date.now();\n      for (const name of names) {\n        const record = this.agents.get(name);\n        if (!record || !this.isStaleIdleAgent(record, now)) continue;\n        if (goalHolders.has(name)) {\n          if (this.goalProtectedIdleSince.get(name) !== record.updatedAt) {\n            this.goalProtectedIdleSince.set(name, record.updatedAt);\n            emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"runtime.agent_reap_skipped\", workerId: name, reason: \"active_goal\" });\n          }\n          continue;\n        }\n        this.goalProtectedIdleSince.delete(name);",

Such an agent ends its turn while the work\n   * it started runs in the background and is woken by that work's\n   * notification. Reaping it killed the agent and its monitors, so the\n   * notification had nothing to wake and the work stalled until the user\n   * noticed. When the lookup fails nothing is reaped: a live agent costs memory,\n   * a reaped one costs the user's work.\n   */\n  private async reapStaleIdleAgents(names: string[]) {\n    if (this.idleReapInFlight) return;\n    this.idleReapInFlight = true;\n    try {\n      let goalHolders: Set<string>;\n      try {\n        goalHolders = await this.findGoalHoldingAgents(names);\n      } catch (error) {\n        process.stderr.write(`[runtime-sweep] goal lookup failed; skipping idle reap: ${describeUnknownError(error)}\\n`);\n        return;\n      }\n      const now = Date.now();\n      for (const name of names) {\n        const record = this.agents.get(name);\n        if (!record || !this.isStaleIdleAgent(record, now)) continue;\n        const skipReason = goalHolders.has(name)\n          ? \"active_goal\"\n          : hasArmedWake(record, now) ? \"armed_wake\" : null;\n        if (skipReason) {\n          if (this.reapSkippedIdleSince.get(name) !== record.updatedAt) {\n            this.reapSkippedIdleSince.set(name, record.updatedAt);\n            emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"runtime.agent_reap_skipped\", workerId: name, reason: skipReason });\n          }\n          continue;\n        }\n        this.reapSkippedIdleSince.delete(name);"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T13:28:14.443Z

I should write tests now: a unit test for readArmedWakeDeadline using real payload shapes, plus a manager-reaper test verifying an agent with a future armedWakeUntil is skipped with "armed_wake" and reaped once it expires. For a more faithful end-to-end check, I'm considering having the stub ACP emit a session/update notification with the Monitor tool_call_update, driven through sessionUpdate.

I need to make sure the stub emits the session/update notification after session/new, but the runtime-client's record isn't set until startAgent completes, so a notification arriving too early would get dropped since the handler returns early if there's no record. I'll just have the stub delay emitting that update by about 50ms after session/new to sidestep the race.

I'm puzzled why the agent got reaped even though waitFor passed and armedWakeUntil should have been set — since reapStaleIdleAgents fetches the same record via the map, the timing must matter: perhaps the reap check ran right before the state was actually set to idle.

I'll fix the test by adjusting the setTimeout delay and idle timeout values so the update always arrives while the record is still fresh, then backdate updatedAt afterward to trigger staleness deliberately.

## Claude · 2026-10-14T13:29:37.613Z

Your three greenhorn workers kept going fine. What died was the Claude session coordinating them, and OmniHarness killed it twice: once through its own idle reaper and once through the nightly restart.

**Timeline (UTC, Oct 14)**
- **02:23:18** The agent armed a 30‑minute Monitor on `transition-polish`, `wave2-journey` and `routing`, then ended its turn. That's how it had been waking itself every 30 minutes all night.
- **02:53:18** The Monitor expired, which should have started its next turn.
- **02:53:22** The runtime's idle reaper killed the agent process (pid 71224, exit 0), four seconds after the expiry. The reaper's idle timeout is also 30 minutes (`OMNIHARNESS_AGENT_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`), so a turn that ends right after arming a 30‑minute Monitor goes stale at almost the same moment the Monitor fires. Earlier wakes that night survived because the reaper's once‑a‑minute sweep happened to land a few seconds later. This time it landed first, and the Monitor and wake went down with the process.
- **03:30** The swap‑watchdog's nightly 05:30 (local) restart restarted OmniHarness.
- **07:10–08:14** The workers finished and wrote their DONE files. Nothing was alive to notice.
- **13:20** You asked "Update?", OmniHarness resumed the saved session, and the agent found everything finished hours earlier.

The reaper already skips agents holding an active `/goal`. This was a direct session with no goal, and OmniHarness had no idea the agent had armed its own wake. The run read as `done` and the worker as `idle`, so the UI showed nothing wrong.

**What I fixed (uncommitted)**
- New `src/server/agent-runtime/acp/armed-wakes.ts` reads completed `Monitor` and `ScheduleWakeup` tool results from the session stream and records when the agent's wake is due. A persistent Monitor counts as armed until the session ends.
- The idle reaper in `manager.ts` now leaves such an agent alone until 2 minutes after the wake is due, and emits `runtime.agent_reap_skipped` with reason `armed_wake`.
- `runtime-client.ts` gets one hook line. Another session has uncommitted edits in that file, so I kept my change to that line and its import.
- Tests: a reaper test that replays this session's exact Monitor payload, plus parser cases. The agent-runtime suite passes (155 tests) and typecheck is clean for the files I touched. The running server is still on the old code until it restarts.

**This fix alone would not have saved last night.** Even if the agent had survived 02:53, the 03:30 restart would have killed it and its re-armed Monitor. Nothing resumes an idle direct session after a restart, so it would still have sat there until you asked. Closing that gap needs:
1. Persist each armed wake (worker, session, due time), not just keep it in memory.
2. When the runtime starts, or if a protected agent dies anyway, resume the saved session at the due time with a prompt like "your monitors were lost in a restart; check your background work and re-arm."
3. Optionally, have the nightly restart skip or defer while an agent has a wake armed.

That work touches the recovery and goal-continuation code another session is editing right now (`recovery*.ts`, `goal-worker-continuation.ts`), which is why I haven't started it. Should I go ahead with it, or commit the reaper fix first?

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:40:26.146Z

I need to implement the durable fix—persisting armed wakes so they resume after restart or process death—and likely commit the reaper fix too. I'm mapping out the architecture first: the agent runtime bridge and app server runner share the same process, since their lock files both point to pid 14189.

There's already a resume path that reconnects a worker from saved session on incoming user messages, and a quota resume path in `worker-resume.ts` that schedules durable supervisor wakes keyed by run and reason, then resumes the session and sends a follow-up prompt at the scheduled time. That's the closest analog to what I need. But I need to check how terminal run status interacts with wake cancellation, since direct runs mark "done" after turn end and that could cancel durable wakes meant for later resumption—I should trace through how the quota resume succeeded for a direct run despite that.

1	import { and, eq, inArray } from "drizzle-orm";
2	import { db, dbClient } from "@/server/db";
3	import { messages, queuedConversationMessages, recoveryIncidents, runs, workers } from "@/server/db/schema";
4	import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
5	import { redactGoalErrorMessage } from "@/server/runs/goal-errors";
6	import { isRuntimeAgentMissingError } from "@/server/workers/runtime-agent-adoption";
7	
8	type RunRecord = typeof runs.$inferSelect;
9	type WorkerRecord = typeof workers.$inferSelect;
10	
11	type ActiveGoalLease = { runId: string; goalId: string; workerId: string };
12	
13	export type GoalContinuationDependencies = {
14	  listPursuedGoals(): Promise<ActiveGoalLease[]>;
15	  loadRun(runId: string): Promise<RunRecord | undefined>;
16	  loadWorker(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerRecord | undefined>;
17	  /** True while a message for the run is still on its way to the worker. */
18	  hasUndeliveredMessages(runId: string): Promise<boolean>;
19	  hasResumableQuotaIncident(runId: string): Promise<boolean>;
20	  /** True while recovery owns the run: an incident that is not yet resolved. */
21	  hasOpenRecoveryIncident(runId: string): Promise<boolean>;
22	  /** Resolves with the runtime agent; rejects when the runtime has none. */
23	  getAgent(workerId: string): Promise<{ state: string }>;
24	  sendContinuation(runId: string, content: string): Promise<unknown>;
25	  now(): number;
26	};
27	
28	/**
29	 * Sent to an agent whose process ended while its goal was still being pursued.
30	 * The resumed session has the transcript but none of the background state the
31	 * agent was waiting on, which is why it has to look again before continuing.
32	 */
33	export const GOAL_CONTINUATION_PROMPT = [
34	  "[OmniHarness] Your previous agent process stopped (a restart, crash or power loss) while your goal was still active, and this session was resumed automatically.",
35	  "Background tasks and monitors from before the stop are gone, so no notification from them will arrive. Detached processes you launched may still be running, or may have died with the machine.",
36	  "Check the real state of that work (processes, logs, git), restart or re-monitor whatever is still needed, and continue pursuing the goal.",
37	].join(" ");
38	
39	/** One continuation per run in this window, so an agent that dies on every resume cannot loop. */
40	const CONTINUATION_COOLDOWN_MS = 10 * 60_000;
41	/**
42	 * Goals the agent finished without reporting it stay `pursuing`. Leaving
43	 * conversations idle this long alone keeps a restart from reviving them.
44	 */
45	const MAX_IDLE_MS = 24 * 60 * 60_000;
46	const BUSY_WORKER_STATUSES = new Set(["starting", "working", "running", "busy", "recovering", "stuck"]);
47	const SKIPPED_WORKER_STATUSES = new Set(["cancelled", "canceled", "cred-exhausted"]);
48	
49	function normalizedStatus(value: string | null | undefined) {
50	  return String(value ?? "").trim().toLowerCase().split(":")[0]?.trim() ?? "";
51	}
52	
53	function lastActivityMs(run: RunRecord, worker: WorkerRecord) {
54	  const stamps = [run.lastActivityAt, worker.updatedAt]
55	    .map((value) => (value instanceof Date ? value.getTime() : NaN))
56	    .filter(Number.isFinite);
57	  return stamps.length > 0 ? Math.max(...stamps) : 0;
58	}
59	
60	const defaultDependencies: GoalContinuationDependencies = {
61	  async listPursuedGoals() {
62	    const result = await dbClient.execute(
63	      `SELECT run_id, goal_id, worker_id FROM run_goals
64	       WHERE status = 'pursuing' AND visible = 1 AND worker_id IS NOT NULL`,
65	    );
66	    return result.rows.map((row) => ({
67	      runId: String(row.run_id),
68	      goalId: String(row.goal_id),
69	      workerId: String(row.worker_id),
70	    }));
71	  },
72	  loadRun: (runId) => db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get(),
73	  loadWorker: (workerId) => db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get(),
74	  async hasUndeliveredMessages(runId) {
75	    const queued = await db.select({ id: queuedConversationMessages.id }).from(queuedConversationMessages).where(and(
76	      eq(queuedConversationMessages.runId, runId),
77	      eq(queuedConversationMessages.status, "pending"),
78	    )).limit(1).get();
79	    if (queued) return true;
80	    const accepted = await db.select({ id: messages.id }).from(messages).where(and(
81	      eq(messages.runId, runId),
82	      inArray(messages.deliveryStatus, ["accepted", "delivering"]),
83	    )).limit(1).get();
84	    return Boolean(accepted);
85	  },
86	  async hasResumableQuotaIncident(runId) {
87	    const { hasResumableQuotaIncident } = await import("@/server/quota/worker-resume");
88	    return hasResumableQuotaIncident(runId);
89	  },
90	  async hasOpenRecoveryIncident(runId) {
91	    const incident = await db.select({ id: recoveryIncidents.id }).from(recoveryIncidents).where(and(
92	      eq(recoveryIncidents.runId, runId),
93	      inArray(recoveryIncidents.status, ["open", "recovering", "needs_user", "failed"]),
94	    )).limit(1).get();
95	    return Boolean(incident);
96	  },
97	  async getAgent(workerId) {
98	    const { getAgent } = await import("@/server/bridge-client");
99	    return getAgent(workerId, { retryIndefinitely: false });
100	  },
101	  async sendContinuation(runId, content) {
102	    const { sendConversationMessage } = await import("@/server/conversations/send-message");
103	    return sendConversationMessage({ runId, content, busyAction: "queue" });
104	  },
105	  now: () => Date.now(),
106	};
107	
108	const lastContinuationAt = new Map<string, number>();
109	
110	/**
111	 * Continue goals whose agent process is gone.
112	 *
113	 * A goal is pursued by an agent process the runtime owns. When that process
114	 * ends without the user stopping it (a runner restart, a crash, the machine
115	 * losing power) nothing else ever starts the agent's next turn: the resumed
116	 * session only runs when someone sends it a message, so the goal sat stalled
117	 * until the user came back and asked why it stopped. This sweep sends that
118	 * message instead.
119	 *
120	 * Only a missing agent counts as stopped. An agent that is alive and idle is
121	 * waiting for its own background work, and the runtime keeps it alive for
122	 * exactly that reason, so it is left alone.
123	 */
124	export async function continueStalledGoals(
125	  dependencies: GoalContinuationDependencies = defaultDependencies,
126	) {
127	  const goals = await dependencies.listPursuedGoals();
128	  let continued = 0;
129	  for (const goal of goals) {
130	    if (await continueStalledGoal(goal, dependencies)) continued += 1;
131	  }
132	  return { continued };
133	}
134	
135	async function continueStalledGoal(goal: ActiveGoalLease, dependencies: GoalContinuationDependencies) {
136	  const now = dependencies.now();
137	  const lastAttempt = lastContinuationAt.get(goal.runId);
138	  if (lastAttempt !== undefined && now - lastAttempt < CONTINUATION_COOLDOWN_MS) return false;
139	
140	  const [run, worker] = await Promise.all([
141	    dependencies.loadRun(goal.runId),
142	    dependencies.loadWorker(goal.workerId),
143	  ]);
144	  if (!run || !worker || worker.runId !== run.id) return false;
145	  if (run.archivedAt) return false;
146	  if (run.mode !== "direct" && run.mode !== "commit") return false;
147	  const runStatus = normalizedStatus(run.status);
148	  if (runStatus === "cancelled" || runStatus === "canceled" || runStatus === "quota_waiting" || runStatus === "needs_recovery") return false;
149	  const workerStatus = normalizedStatus(worker.status);
150	  if (BUSY_WORKER_STATUSES.has(workerStatus) || SKIPPED_WORKER_STATUSES.has(workerStatus)) return false;
151	  if (!worker.bridgeSessionId?.trim()) return false;
152	  if (now - lastActivityMs(run, worker) > MAX_IDLE_MS) return false;
153	  if (await dependencies.hasUndeliveredMessages(run.id)) return false;
154	  if (await dependencies.hasResumableQuotaIncident(run.id)) return false;
155	  // Recovery owns a run with an open incident. Sending past it queued a
156	  // continuation that could only fail against the missing agent, and each
157	  // failure refreshed the worker's activity stamp, so the sweep re-sent one
158	  // every cooldown for as long as the incident stayed open.
159	  if (await dependencies.hasOpenRecoveryIncident(run.id)) return false;
160	
161	  try {
162	    const agent = await dependencies.getAgent(worker.id);
163	    const agentState = normalizedStatus(agent.state);
164	    if (agentState !== "stopped" && agentState !== "error") return false;
165	  } catch (error) {
166	    // Anything but a definite "no such agent" (the runtime still starting, a
167	    // dropped connection) is retried on the next sweep.
168	    if (!isRuntimeAgentMissingError(error)) return false;
169	  }
170	
171	  lastContinuationAt.set(goal.runId, now);
172	  emitNamedEvent({
173	    kind: "goal.worker_continuation.started",
174	    runId: goal.runId,
175	    goalId: goal.goalId,
176	    workerId: worker.id,
177	  });
178	  try {
179	    await dependencies.sendContinuation(run.id, GOAL_CONTINUATION_PROMPT);
180	    emitNamedEvent({
181	      kind: "goal.worker_continuation.completed",
182	      runId: goal.runId,
183	      goalId: goal.goalId,
184	      workerId: worker.id,
185	    });
186	    return true;
187	  } catch (error) {
188	    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
189	    emitNamedEvent({
190	      kind: "goal.worker_continuation.failed",
191	      runId: goal.runId,
192	      goalId: goal.goalId,
193	      workerId: worker.id,
194	      reason,
195	    });
196	    emitNamedEvent({
197	      kind: "error.surfaced",
198	      code: "goal.continuation.failed",
199	      message: `The goal's agent stopped and could not be resumed automatically: ${reason}`,
200	      surface: "banner",
201	      runId: goal.runId,
202	      workerId: worker.id,
203	    });
204	    return false;
205	  }
206	}
207	
208	export function __resetGoalContinuationForTests() {
209	  lastContinuationAt.clear();
210	}
211

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:40:56.170Z

This confirms the model I want: a sweep that continues goals whose agent has disappeared, and the relevant file is mid-edit in another session. I should check the git diff to see what's in progress and locate where continueStalledGoals gets invoked for the sweep cadence. My design is "armed wake continuation" - I need to figure out persistence, since when runtime-client records an armed wake it needs a durable place to store it, like a new column on workers for armed_wake_until.

That would require a schema migration, so I should check how migrations work given the drizzle schema and whether db/index.ts has a custom ensure-schema step with ALTER TABLE statements. An alternative is deriving this from the worker's persisted JSONL stream instead of a DB column, but scanning JSONL across all workers in a sweep is messy, so a DB column seems cleaner. For the write path, I need to confirm whether runtime-client has DB access in the same process, since the goal-state handler already writes to DB from runtime-client via goalControl, so this should work similarly even though the runtime is conceptually a separate bridge.

I should find where the app-side ingests entries (appendWorkerEntry callers) since that's the better place to persist "armed wake" onto the workers row. For the continuation sweep logic, I'm thinking through when to resume a worker: if armed_wake_until is set and the agent is missing or stopped, I should resume immediately rather than waiting for the due time, since the agent's background work could finish anytime and it needs to re-arm as soon as possible. The condition would be: armed wake pending and not yet consumed, plus agent missing, triggers an immediate continuation with a prompt reminding the agent to check background work and re-arm.

I'm now working out how the wake gets cleared — the pending state should last while now is less than armed_wake_until plus grace, and clear once the agent runs a new turn and either re-arms with a later deadline or doesn't. For persistent monitors with an infinite deadline, I need a sentinel value like a very large integer stored in SQLite, but capped by the same 24-hour MAX_IDLE window used for goals so an agent doesn't get revived indefinitely after the user's work is done.

Checking the edge cases: after restart, the sweep still sees the worker idle with a pending armed wake but no running agent, so it proceeds correctly. A later re-arm just extends the deadline, which is still fine as a current pending wake, and a new user message doesn't invalidate an in-process monitor's deadline either. The reaper won't act until deadline-plus-grace, so the only real gap is if the agent's process genuinely misses firing the wake.

I want to avoid needing to detect turn starts on the app side to clear a stored wake value, since that's fragile — simpler to just treat the wake as pending as long as now is before the stored deadline plus grace.

Now I need to find where continueStalledGoals is invoked, since that's where the equivalent armed-wake continuation logic should hook in, likely at boot time to cover the restart case. The reaper reaping after deadline+grace with no new activity is correct behavior and doesn't need changing. I'm weighing whether to add a sibling function alongside continueStalledGoals or create a separate module, since the goal module's defaults aren't exported and the file already has unrelated edits in progress from another session.

The cleanest approach is generalizing continueStalledGoals so it also considers armed-wake leases, listing candidates from both goals and armed wakes with a distinct prompt — but that touches a file mid-flux, so I should check the git diff to understand the scope of the other edits first. On the nightly restart deferral item, restart-control.ts is also dirty, and since continuation after restart handles things fine, I can skip that deferral. For the in-memory armedWakeUntil state, after a restart a resumed agent just gets the continuation prompt and re-arms itself, which is fine. Next I want to check the callers and diff to confirm all this.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:41:12.237Z

Since the other session's edits look small and stable, I want to generalize this module so the candidate list covers both pursued goals and workers with a pending armed wake, using a distinct prompt for the armed wake case. To avoid colliding with the existing goal.worker_continuation.* events, I'm leaning toward creating a separate module for armed-wake continuation with its own started/completed/failed events rather than overloading the existing ones.

I'm weighing whether to refactor continueStalledGoal's eligibility checks into a shared exported function versus just duplicating the ~40 lines, since editing the shared file risks conflicting with the other session's concurrent changes. Since the Edit tool applies atomically to current content, the real danger isn't code conflicts but commit hygiene — if I touch a shared file, my commit could sweep in their unrelated WIP changes or vice versa. Given that this user runs multiple sessions in parallel, I'll lean toward keeping my changes isolated to files that are wholly mine and accept some duplication to avoid entangling commits.

Now I'm thinking through where to persist armedWakeUntil durably — either adding a new workers table column or writing to the DB directly from the runtime-client — and tracing how worker status updates flow in from the runtime to figure out the right integration point.

I'll use Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER to represent Infinity in both memory and storage, since the existing MAX_IDLE_MS check during continuation still bounds revival to 24h of inactivity, and in-memory reaper protection for persistent monitors is effectively forever anyway. Now I need to check db/index.ts for the migration.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:41:46.886Z

I should check the watchdog sweep cadence at the top of the file to see the interval—probably runs periodically and on boot, which seems fine. Now I'm reworking the continuation module to generalize the goal-worker-continuation logic so it handles two kinds of leases, one for goal-based reasons and one for armed-wake reasons with a wake-until timestamp.

Actually this would touch the dependencies type and break existing test mocks since adding a new dependency like listing armed wake workers isn't optional in their typed interface. A cleaner approach is a separate armed-wake-continuation module with its own dependencies, extracting the shared eligibility check as an exported function that takes a subset of the goal continuation dependencies—loading runs, workers, checking undelivered messages and recovery incidents—to determine if a worker is stopped and continuable. I also need to handle cooldown tracking and avoid double-continuing a run that has both a pursued goal and an armed wake, probably by having the armed-wake sweep skip runs already owned by the goal sweep via a SQL exclusion.

I'm drafting the resume prompt for armed-wake continuations, explaining that the previous process stopped while waiting on an armed wake, so the agent should check real state and re-arm monitoring. For clearing state after continuation, the column should reset once the wake effectively fires so it doesn't re-trigger, with the cooldown preventing loops for ten minutes until a new deadline gets written if the agent re-arms.

Better to explicitly null out armed_wake_until when the continuation is sent, so only a fresh re-arm re-enables the wake rather than leaving the stale deadline active. I'm also checking edge cases like worker cancellation and the 24-hour idle timeout, and verifying the deadline-plus-grace math against the actual restart timeline to confirm the fix behaves correctly.

But if the machine was off for hours due to power loss, now exceeds the deadline with no continuation — yet that missed wake should still count, since the agent died before the wake ever fired. The real test should be whether the agent's process stopped while a wake was still pending, which I could approximate by comparing the armed wake deadline to the worker's last recorded activity timestamp, assuming that timestamp updates on each turn.

So I'll store the due time without grace as `armed_wake_at` in the DB, keep the grace period only in runtime memory, and treat the continuation condition as: agent missing AND wake_due exceeds last activity, bounded by the 24h max-idle cutoff. This also elegantly covers the case where the reaper kills a stuck agent after a wake silently failed to fire, since the sweep would still see the wake as unconsumed relative to last activity.

Now I want to confirm whether worker.updatedAt is actually updated reliably on every turn, including unprompted task-notification turns—checking this worker's updated_at timestamp against last activity, and tracing through the sync code to see when workers.updated_at gets written versus when the runtime just flips state to "working" without syncing the DB.

/**
 * claude-agent-acp attaches `cost` to a `usage_update` only when the SDK
 * reports a turn `result` — for prompted turns and for the ones Claude starts
 * on its own after a background task notification alike.
 */
function isTurnResultUsageUpdate(update: Record<string, unknown>) {
  return finiteNumber(asRecord(update.cost)?.amount) !== null;
}

/**
 * Claude has no Codex-style thread status, so a turn it starts on its own
 * (a background-task notification) reads as working from its first tool call
 * and, without this, never reads as idle again. The stuck-worker reaper then
 * took the quiet "working" worker for a hung one and re-sent the user's last,
 * already-answered message — which the agent read as fresh consent to act.
 */
function settleUnpromptedTurnAtResult(record: AgentRecord, update: Record<string, unknown>) {
  if (!isTurnResultUsageUpdate(update)) return;
  // `askAgent` settles the turn it started, and a Codex /goal turn ends on
  // its own thread status.
  if (record.promptInFlight || record.providerTurnActive) return;
  if (record.state !== "working") return;
  if (record.pendingPermissions.length > 0 || record.pendingElicitations.length > 0) return;
  endProviderTurn(record);
}

function applySessionUsageUpdate(record: AgentRecord, update: Record<string, unknown>) {
  const used = finiteNumber(update.used);
  const size = finiteNumber(update.size);
  if (used === null || size === null || size <= 0) {
    return false;
  }

  updateContextUsage(record, {
    totalTokens: used,
    maxTokens: size,
    fullnessPercent: Math.min(100, Math.max(0, (used / size) * 100)),
  });
  return true;
}

function stripAgentControlText(text: string) {
  return text.replace(/^\[MODE_UPDATE\]\s*autoEdit/i, "");

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:42:15.417Z

I'm unsure whether worker.updatedAt gets touched by unprompted turns, so instead I'm considering tracking "consumed" explicitly in the DB — persisting an armed_wake_at time and having the runtime clear or overwrite it when it detects actual turn activity like message chunks or tool calls.

With multiple monitors, I only need to store the max due time, since activity at a given point only "consumes" the wake if it's at or after that max due time — otherwise an earlier-due monitor firing shouldn't clear a later one. But monitor loop events fire continuously before expiry, so the due stays stale until expiry or until the agent explicitly stops the monitor, meaning a restart could keep running a stopped monitor at most until its stale due time.

The low cost of a false-positive continuation (just a polite prompt) outweighs the risk of a stalled session, so persistent monitors with an infinite due are acceptable to revive within the 24h idle bound. Power-loss and reaper-after-grace cases are both handled since the sweep checks for an unconsumed wake plus a missing agent, and on restart the continuation resumes the session via message and clears the DB column to prevent repeat loops.

Even an early wake-up after restart is fine since the re-arm prompt lets the agent recheck state and reset its wait. For activity detection, I'm placing the arming/consuming logic in a shared helper that tracks whether an agent turn should clear an armed wake based on current time versus due, keeping in-memory fields for due time and persisting changes through a callback.

Now I'm working through the edge case where a Monitor notification fires before the wake is actually due — that shouldn't consume the armed wake, only an actual post-due turn output should. For persistence, I'm adding a database column for the armed wake timestamp and need a per-worker write-ordering mechanism so that a "clear" write followed quickly by a "set new value" write can't race and get persisted out of order.

On failure handling, I need an error-surfaced code like "worker.armed_wake.persist_failed", plus a named event such as runtime.armed_wake_changed capturing the worker id and new wakeAt value, satisfying the rule that every decision emits an event. I'm also considering whether the runtime-client tests that exercise manager-reaper logic actually hit a real database when persisting, since similar goal-state handlers get called under test too — so I may need to inject the dependency rather than importing the db module directly.

Since runtime-client doesn't have manager-level options like findGoalHoldingAgents, I'm weighing whether to let the manager handle persistence itself or pass an optional callback through the runtime-client constructor, and I want to check how runtime-client is actually instantiated by the manager to see if such options already flow through.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:42:40.015Z

An alternative is to persist from the manager's periodic reap sweep instead of wiring it through the runtime-client: every 60s, compare each record's armedWakeAt against lastPersisted and write only the changes. That avoids extra wiring, batches naturally, and could be made testable with a `persistArmedWakes?` option — though it introduces up to 60s of latency, meaning a restart shortly after arming could lose that state, which seems like a minor, acceptable tradeoff unless shutdown isn't always graceful.

I'll settle on a sweep-based `syncArmedWakes()` in the manager, invoked during the reap sweep, with a test override `persistArmedWake?: (workerId, wakeAt|null) => Promise<void>` whose default implementation updates the workers table's armed_wake_at column by id — rows for non-DB worker names just affect zero rows harmlessly. I'll track lastPersistedArmedWake per worker name, and importantly, removing an agent from the active set won't clear its DB value, since surviving the agent's death is the whole point. On restart, resumed records start with armedWakeAt undefined, so I should only write when it's explicitly set and differs from what's persisted, treating undefined as "unknown, skip."

Still, there's a gap: a resumed worker with a stale DB armed_wake_at won't naturally clear it since the new process has no in-memory state to trigger consumption, so a later sweep could wrongly continue the agent even after the user re-engaged. The cleanest fix is to have the first turn of a new process explicitly write null to the DB if armedWakeAt is undefined, signaling that any old wake is now moot and the agent is actively running again — any future arming would then produce a fresh value.

Now I'm working through the reap-and-continuation path: if a process is killed mid-wake, the sweep notices the agent missing, continues it, and clears the stored value. I'm also detailing the in-memory consumption logic — turn outputs clear armedWakeAt when it's undefined or already past due, while tool-result deadlines arm it to the max of the current and new deadline, and the reaper treats a wake as active only if the current time is still within the deadline plus grace period. But I'm realizing there's still an edge case with consuming a turn output at a specific time that needs more thought.

Checking the reap-race timing against the monitor expiry and wake turn, I'm confirming persistent monitors stay infinite and never get consumed. I'm renaming armedWakeUntil to armedWakeAt and planning to update the tests, then sketching the continuation module that lists workers with an armed wake set, skipping any that are already held by an active goal sweep.

For eligibility, I want to share logic with the goal continuation path by extracting a reusable function in the goal-worker-continuation module that checks cooldowns and returns the run/worker pair if continuable, plus a helper to mark when continuation last happened so both paths stay consistent. After sending the continuation, I need to clear the armed_wake_at field in the database.

I should also emit lifecycle events for the armed wake continuation (started/completed/failed) with a proper error code, and hook this into the runtime-watchdog right after continueStalledGoals runs on its interval. I need to double check edge cases: boot-time retries if the runtime is still starting, workers idle beyond the 24h max that shouldn't be woken, and confirming the continuation prompt shows up consistently as a user message in the UI just like goal continuation does.

Now I want to start writing the actual code — refactoring the goal module by extracting the shared logic out of continueStalledGoal into its own function.

Shared by every sweep that resumes a stopped agent,\n * so they agree on what counts as stopped and share one cooldown per run.\n */\nexport async function findContinuableStoppedWorker(\n  lease: { runId: string; workerId: string },\n  dependencies: StoppedWorkerDependencies,\n) {\n  const now = dependencies.now();\n  const lastAttempt = lastContinuationAt.get(lease.runId);\n  if (lastAttempt !== undefined && now - lastAttempt < CONTINUATION_COOLDOWN_MS) return null;\n\n  const [run, worker] = await Promise.all([\n    dependencies.loadRun(lease.runId),\n    dependencies.loadWorker(lease.workerId),\n  ]);\n  if (!run || !worker || worker.runId !== run.id) return null;\n  return await isStoppedAndContinuable(run, worker, now, dependencies) ? worker : null;\n}\n\nexport function markWorkerContinued(runId: string, now: number) {\n  lastContinuationAt.set(runId, now);\n}\n\nasync function isStoppedAndContinuable(\n  run: RunRecord,\n  worker: WorkerRecord,\n  now: number,\n  dependencies: StoppedWorkerDependencies,\n) {"

```text
  },
  async sendContinuation(runId, content) {
    const { sendConversationMessage } = await import("@/server/conversations/send-message");
    return sendConversationMessage({ runId, content, busyAction: "queue" });
  },
  now: () => Date.now(),
};

const lastContinuationAt = new Map<string, number>();

/**
 * Continue goals whose agent process is gone.
 *
 * A goal is pursued by an agent process the runtime owns. When that process
 * ends without the user stopping it (a runner restart, a crash, the machine
 * losing power) nothing else ever starts the agent's next turn: the resumed
 * session only runs when someone sends it a message, so the goal sat stalled
 * until the user came back and asked why it stopped. This sweep sends that
 * message instead.
 *
 * Only a missing agent counts as stopped. An agent that is alive and idle is
 * waiting for its own background work, and the runtime keeps it alive for
 * exactly that reason, so it is left alone.
 */
export async function continueStalledGoals(
  dependencies: GoalContinuationDependencies = defaultDependencies,
) {
  const goals = await dependencies.listPursuedGoals();
  let continued = 0;
  for (const goal of goals) {
    if (await continueStalledGoal(goal, dependencies)) continued += 1;
  }
  return { continued };
}

export type StoppedWorkerDependencies = Omit<GoalContinuationDependencies, "listPursuedGoals" | "sendContinuation">;

{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/acp/armed-wakes.ts",
  "content": "import type { AgentRecord } from \"../types\";\n\n/**\n * How long past an armed wake's due time the agent stays protected from the\n * idle reaper. The wake starts a turn inside the agent, and only that turn's\n * first output reaches the runtime; until then the agent still reads as idle.\n */\nexport const ARMED_WAKE_GRACE_MS = 2 * 60_000;\n\n/** Stored for a persistent Monitor, which can fire until the session ends. */\nexport const PERSISTENT_WAKE_AT = Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER;\n\ntype ArmedWakeRecord = Pick<AgentRecord, \"armedWakeAt\">;\n\nfunction asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {\n  return value && typeof value === \"object\" && !Array.isArray(value)\n    ? value as Record<string, unknown>\n    : null;\n}\n\nfunction positiveNumber(value: unknown) {\n  return typeof value === \"number\" && Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0 ? value : null;\n}\n\n/**\n * When the agent will next wake itself, from a completed Claude Code `Monitor`\n * or `ScheduleWakeup` tool call.\n */\nexport function readArmedWakeDeadline(update: unknown, now: number): number | null {\n  const record = asRecord(update);\n  if (!record || record.sessionUpdate !== \"tool_call_update\") return null;\n  const claudeCode = asRecord(asRecord(record._meta)?.claudeCode);\n  const response = asRecord(claudeCode?.toolResponse);\n  if (!response) return null;\n  if (claudeCode?.toolName === \"Monitor\") {\n    if (response.persistent === true) return PERSISTENT_WAKE_AT;\n    const timeoutMs = positiveNumber(response.timeoutMs);\n    return timeoutMs === null ? null : now + timeoutMs;\n  }\n  if (claudeCode?.toolName === \"ScheduleWakeup\") {\n    return positiveNumber(response.scheduledFor);\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\nfunction isTurnOutput(update: unknown) {\n  const kind = asRecord(update)?.sessionUpdate;\n  return kind === \"agent_message_chunk\" || kind === \"agent_thought_chunk\" || kind === \"tool_call\";\n}\n\n/**\n * Track the wake an agent armed for itself. Such an agent ends its turn and\n * waits for the wake to start the next one, so it must not be reaped before\n * then, and if its process dies first something else has to start that turn.\n *\n * Arming keeps the latest due time. Turn output at or after the due time means\n * the wake ran, so it is consumed. The first output of a process that never saw\n * an arming (a fresh or resumed session) consumes whatever an earlier process\n * armed: that process, and its monitors, are gone.\n */\nexport function trackArmedWake(record: ArmedWakeRecord, update: unknown, now = Date.now()) {\n  if (isTurnOutput(update)) {\n    if (record.armedWakeAt === undefined || (record.armedWakeAt !== null && now >= record.armedWakeAt)) {\n      record.armedWakeAt = null;\n    }\n    return;\n  }\n  const deadline = readArmedWakeDeadline(update, now);\n  if (deadline !== null && (record.armedWakeAt ?? 0) < deadline) record.armedWakeAt = deadline;\n}\n\nexport function hasArmedWake(record: ArmedWakeRecord, now: number) {\n  return typeof record.armedWakeAt === \"number\" && now < record.armedWakeAt + ARMED_WAKE_GRACE_MS;\n}\n"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:43:21.665Z

I'm adding persistence to the reap sweep manager, introducing an optional async callback to persist armed wake times per worker, tracking the persisted value in a map, and emitting a named event when it changes. If persistence fails, I surface it as a logged error with a dedicated code so failures aren't silent but also don't block the sweep.

Thinking through timing edge cases — should persistence happen immediately or only on the 60s sweep cadence? Tracing through scenarios like rearming within the same sweep window or a restart occurring between consumption and the next sweep, the sweep-based approach stays conservative since the DB retains the prior due time and the agent continues rather than silently losing the wake. Now I'll locate the manager options type near line 1053 to add the new field there.



uuid: 5cd7b1dc-2253-47ea-94a0-035c4fde253a
parent: cfae3081-5278-4a13-a264-b9599f24280a

The runtime\n   * holds the wake only in memory, so a restart or crash lost both the wake and\n   * any record that the agent was waiting on one; the continuation sweep reads\n   * this to start the turn the wake would have.\n   */\n  private async syncArmedWakes() {\n    if (this.armedWakeSyncInFlight) return;\n    this.armedWakeSyncInFlight = true;\n    try {\n      for (const [name, record] of this.agents) {\n        const wakeAt = record.armedWakeAt;\n        if (wakeAt === undefined || this.persistedArmedWakeAt.get(name) === wakeAt) continue;\n        try {\n          await this.persistArmedWake(name, wakeAt);\n          this.persistedArmedWakeAt.set(name, wakeAt);\n          emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"runtime.armed_wake_persisted\", workerId: name, wakeAt });\n        } catch (error) {\n          emitNamedEvent({\n            kind: \"error.surfaced\",\n            code: \"worker.armed_wake.persist_failed\",\n            message: `Could not record the wake this agent armed; it will not be resumed if it stops first: ${describeUnknownError(error)}`,\n            surface: \"log\",\n            workerId: name,\n          });\n        }\n      }\n      for (const name of this.persistedArmedWakeAt.keys()) {\n        if (!this.agents.has(name)) this.persistedArmedWakeAt.delete(name);\n      }\n    } finally {\n      this.armedWakeSyncInFlight = false;\n    }\n  }\n\n  private async findGoalHoldingAgents(names: readonly string[]) {''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF",

  "content": "import { eq, isNotNull } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { db } from \"@/server/db\";\nimport { withSqliteBusyRetry } from \"@/server/db/retry\";\nimport { workers } from \"@/server/db/schema\";\nimport { emitNamedEvent } from \"@/server/events/named-events\";\nimport { redactGoalErrorMessage } from \"@/server/runs/goal-errors\";\nimport {\n  defaultContinuationDependencies,\n  findContinuableStoppedWorker,\n  markWorkerContinued,\n  type GoalContinuationDependencies,\n} from \"@/server/runs/goal-worker-continuation\";\n\ntype ArmedWakeLease = { runId: string; workerId: string; wakeAt: number };\n\nexport type ArmedWakeContinuationDependencies = Omit<GoalContinuationDependencies, \"listPursuedGoals\"> & {\n  listArmedWakes(): Promise<ArmedWakeLease[]>;\n  /** Workers whose goal the goal sweep already continues. */\n  listGoalHolders(workerIds: readonly string[]): Promise<Set<string>>;\n  clearArmedWake(workerId: string): Promise<void>;\n};\n\n/**\n * Sent to an agent whose process ended before a wake it armed could run. The\n * resumed session has the transcript but not the monitors, which is why it has\n * to look again instead of waiting for a notification.\n */\nexport const ARMED_WAKE_CONTINUATION_PROMPT = [\n  \"[OmniHarness] Your previous agent process stopped (a restart, crash or power loss) while you were waiting on a wake you had armed (a Monitor or ScheduleWakeup), and this session was resumed automatically.\",\n  \"That wake and every other background task or monitor from before the stop are gone, so no notification from them will arrive. Detached processes you launched may still be running, or may have finished while you were stopped.\",\n  \"Check the real state of that work (processes, logs, git), act on whatever finished, re-arm any monitoring that is still needed, and continue.\",\n].join(\" \");\n\nexport async function persistWorkerArmedWake(workerId: string, wakeAt: number | null) {\n  await withSqliteBusyRetry(() => db.update(workers).set({ armedWakeAt: wakeAt }).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)));\n}\n\nconst defaultDependencies: ArmedWakeContinuationDependencies = {\n  ...defaultContinuationDependencies,\n  async listArmedWakes() {\n    const rows = await db\n      .select({ runId: workers.runId, workerId: workers.id, wakeAt: workers.armedWakeAt })\n      .from(workers)\n      .where(isNotNull(workers.armedWakeAt));\n    return rows.flatMap((row) => (row.wakeAt === null ? [] : [{ ...row, wakeAt: row.wakeAt }]));\n  },\n  async listGoalHolders(workerIds) {\n    const { listWorkersHoldingActiveGoals } = await import(\"@/server/runs/goal-worker-lease\");\n    return listWorkersHoldingActiveGoals(workerIds);\n  },\n  clearArmedWake: (workerId) => persistWorkerArmedWake(workerId, null),\n};\n\n/**\n * Resume agents whose process died before a wake they armed could run.\n *\n * An agent coordinating background work ends its turn after arming a Monitor\n * or ScheduleWakeup and relies on it to start the next turn. Both live inside\n * the agent process, so when the runtime restarted, or reaped the agent, the\n * wake died with it and the conversation sat idle until the user asked why.\n * This sweep starts that turn instead. The goal sweep owns agents holding a\n * goal, so they are left to it.\n */\nexport async function continueArmedWakeWorkers(\n  dependencies: ArmedWakeContinuationDependencies = defaultDependencies,\n) {\n  const leases = await dependencies.listArmedWakes();\n  if (leases.length === 0) return { continued: 0 };\n  const goalHolders = await dependencies.listGoalHolders(leases.map((lease) => lease.workerId));\n  let continued = 0;\n  for (const lease of leases) {\n    if (goalHolders.has(lease.workerId)) continue;\n    if (await continueArmedWakeWorker(lease, dependencies)) continued += 1;\n  }\n  return { continued };\n}\n\nasync function continueArmedWakeWorker(lease: ArmedWakeLease, dependencies: ArmedWakeContinuationDependencies) {\n  const worker = await findContinuableStoppedWorker(lease, dependencies);\n  if (!worker) return false;\n\n  markWorkerContinued(lease.runId, dependencies.now());\n  emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"worker.armed_wake_continuation.started\", ...lease });\n  try {\n    await dependencies.sendContinuation(lease.runId, ARMED_WAKE_CONTINUATION_PROMPT);\n    // The continuation is the turn the wake would have started.\n    await dependencies.clearArmedWake(lease.workerId);\n    emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"worker.armed_wake_continuation.completed\", ...lease });\n    return true;\n  } catch (error) {\n    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);\n    emitNamedEvent({ kind: \"worker.armed_wake_continuation.failed\", ...lease, reason });\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"error.surfaced\",\n      code: \"worker.armed_wake_continuation.failed\",\n      message: `The agent stopped while waiting on a wake it armed and could not be resumed automatically: ${reason}`,\n      surface: \"banner\",\n      runId: lease.runId,\n      workerId: lease.workerId,\n    });\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:44:07.414Z

I'm double-checking a few edge cases: the resumed agent's wake timestamp handling falls back to null safely, the idle check correctly relies on lastActivityAt, status "done" doesn't block goal continuation, and the watchdog does run in-process at boot.

Then I think about worker status after reaping — likely stays "idle" rather than switching to a skip state like cancelled or cred-exhausted, which matches the incident. For tests, I'm planning updates to armed-wakes for the new trackArmedWake semantics, a manager-reaper test covering the field rename and conditional persistence, and an armed-wake-continuation test covering missing/alive/goal-holder agents, wake clearing on send, and failure handling — using the existing goal-worker-continuation test's mock patterns as a reference.

import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { __resetNamedEventsForTests, getNamedEventsSince } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import {
  __resetGoalContinuationForTests,
  continueStalledGoals,
  GOAL_CONTINUATION_PROMPT,
  type GoalContinuationDependencies,
} from "@/server/runs/goal-worker-continuation";

const NOW = Date.parse("2026-10-05T19:00:00.000Z");

type Overrides = {
  run?: Record<string, unknown>;
  worker?: Record<string, unknown>;
  agent?: { state: string } | Error;
  undelivered?: boolean;
  quotaIncident?: boolean;
  recoveryIncident?: boolean;
  send?: () => Promise<unknown>;
  now?: () => number;
};

function dependencies(overrides: Overrides = {}) {
  return {
    listPursuedGoals: vi.fn(async () => [{ runId: "run-1", goalId: "goal-1", workerId: "worker-1" }]),
    loadRun: vi.fn(async () => ({
      id: "run-1",
      mode: "direct",
      status: "done",
      archivedAt: null,
      lastActivityAt: new Date(NOW - 60 * 60_000),
      ...overrides.run,
    }) as never),
    loadWorker: vi.fn(async () => ({
      id: "worker-1",
      runId: "run-1",
      status: "idle",
      bridgeSessionId: "session-1",
      updatedAt: new Date(NOW - 60 * 60_000),
      ...overrides.worker,
    }) as never),
    hasUndeliveredMessages: vi.fn(async () => overrides.undelivered ?? false),
    hasResumableQuotaIncident: vi.fn(async () => overrides.quotaIncident ?? false),
    hasOpenRecoveryIncident: vi.fn(async () => overrides.recoveryIncident ?? false),
    getAgent: vi.fn(async () => {
      const agent = overrides.agent ?? new Error("Get agent failed: 404 Agent not found: worker-1");
      if (agent instanceof Error) throw agent;
      return agent;
    }),
    sendContinuation: vi.fn(overrides.send ?? (async () => ({ ok: true }))),
    now: overrides.now ?? (() => NOW),
  } satisfies GoalContinuationDependencies;
}

function eventKinds() {
  return getNamedEventsSince(0).events.map((entry) => entry.event.kind);
}

describe("continueStalledGoals", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    __resetNamedEventsForTests();
    __resetGoalContinuationForTests();
  });

  // fc8d9cb681ef: the runner restarted (power loss) and the goal sat stalled
  // for three hours until the user asked "are you stuck".
  it("continues a pursued goal whose agent process is gone", async () => {
    const deps = dependencies();

    await expect(continueStalledGoals(deps)).resolves.toEqual({ continued: 1 });

    expect(deps.sendContinuation).toHaveBeenCalledWith("run-1", GOAL_CONTINUATION_PROMPT);
    expect(eventKinds()).toEqual(["goal.worker_continuation.started", "goal.worker_continuation.completed"]);
  });

  it("treats a stopped runtime record as a gone agent", async () => {
    const deps = dependencies({ agent: { state: "stopped" } });

    await expect(continueStalledGoals(deps)).resolves.toEqual({ continued: 1 });
  });

  it("leaves a live idle agent alone, since it is waiting for its own background work", async () => {
    const deps = dependencies({ agent: { state: "idle" } });

    await expect(continueStalledGoals(deps)).resolves.toEqual({ continued: 0 });
    expect(deps.sendContinuation).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("retries later when the runtime cannot answer yet", async () => {
    const deps = dependencies({ agent: new Error("connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:7800") });

```text
import fs from "fs";
import os from "os";
import path from "path";
import { beforeEach } from "vitest";

// Never trust an inherited runner root here. Agent processes launched by a
// live OmniHarness runner inherit its OMNIHARNESS_ROOT; respecting that value
// would let ordinary test cleanup operate on the production database.
const testRoot = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "omniharness-tests-"));
process.env.OMNIHARNESS_ROOT = testRoot;

// Same hazard, one variable family further out. `applyProjectScopedCliStorage`
// deliberately yields to a caller-supplied CLI storage dir, and Claude worker
// startup yields to a caller-supplied ANTHROPIC_MODEL. Tests that assert the
// runtime pins storage under the project root, or pins an exact model, spread
// `...process.env` into the runtime they spawn — so whenever the suite runs
// from a shell that exports these (notably inside an agent CLI, which sets
// CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR and ANTHROPIC_MODEL for its own session), the runtime took
// the yield branch and those tests failed on the developer's machine while
// passing in CI. Scrub them once, here, so no test can inherit them.
for (const key of [
  "ANTHROPIC_MODEL",
  "CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL",
  "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR",
  "CODEX_HOME",
  "CODEX_SQLITE_HOME",
  "GEMINI_CLI_HOME",
  "OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR",
  "XDG_CACHE_HOME",
  "XDG_DATA_HOME",
  "XDG_STATE_HOME",
]) {
  delete process.env[key];
}

  const workerId = `${runId}-worker-1`;

    id: planId,

    status: "quota_waiting",
    createdAt: now,
    updatedAt: now,
  });

    id: workerId,

    createdAt: now,

  await db.insert(recoveryIncidents).values({
    id: incidentId,

    workerId,
    kind: "quota_exhausted",
    status: "open",
    autoAttemptCount: 0,
    details: JSON.stringify({ recoveryState: "quota_waiting" }),
    detectedAt: now,
    updatedAt: now,
  });
  const run = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();
  return { runId, workerId, incidentId, run: run! };
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-14T21:46:48.188Z

I'm going through file by file: manager.ts is entirely mine, while runtime-client.ts and named-events.ts have a mix of my changes and theirs. The goal-worker-continuation.ts file looks the most heavily mixed, with their side adding a recoveryIncidents import and a hasOpenRecoveryIncident dependency I need to reconcile with my edits.

Since my refactor incorporates their hasOpenRecoveryIncident check directly, separating the changes cleanly isn't really feasible — my version depends on theirs. I'm weighing whether to commit the whole file including their unfinished work, since it's coherent and its tests pass, versus just leaving everything uncommitted and flagging it for the user, given that committing someone else's WIP feels presumptuous and wasn't explicitly requested.

I'm continuing the same approach for runtime-client.ts and named-events.ts, then thinking through whether to verify the staged blobs by checking them out to a temp directory and running tsc/tests there. It's heavy, but I want to decide whether restarting matters here — the runner picks up working-tree code via tsx, so a restart isn't my call anyway. I'm leaning toward committing precisely with verified index blobs if time allows.

Decided: HEAD plus my changes stays consistent with the working tree, so I'll go ahead with staging. I'm working out which files are purely mine to stage wholesale (new files, manager.ts, types.ts, db/index.ts, schema.ts, watchdog, tests) versus mixed files like runtime-client.ts, named-events.ts, and goal-worker-continuation.ts that need careful hunk selection.

Once I've assembled the staged content, I'll export to a temp directory, link node_modules, and run tsc plus vitest against my tests there to validate everything before committing.

## Claude · 2026-10-14T21:48:21.279Z

Done and committed as `68d1b486`. It isn't pushed, and it takes effect the next time OmniHarness restarts: the 05:30 nightly restart, or sooner if you restart it yourself.

**What changed**
- **Reaper:** an agent that has armed a Monitor or ScheduleWakeup is no longer reaped until 2 minutes after the wake is due. That alone would have stopped the 02:53 kill.
- **Restart and crash survival:** the wake's due time is now saved in a new `workers.armed_wake_at` column. A watchdog sweep (every 15s) looks for agents whose process is gone and whose wake never ran. It resumes the saved session with a message saying the monitors were lost, asking it to check its background work, act on whatever finished and re-arm. In your case, the 05:30 restart would have triggered that within seconds, while the greenhorn workers were still running.
- **When a wake counts as done:** a turn that runs at or after the due time consumes the wake, and so does a continuation the sweep sends. Monitor events that arrive earlier don't consume it. A fresh or resumed process forgets wakes armed by the process before it, so an old wake can't wake the agent twice.
- **Overlap with goals:** this reuses the goal sweep's checks for "is this worker really stopped" and its one-per-10-minutes limit per run. Agents holding a `/goal` are left to the goal sweep, so nothing gets two continuations.
- **Failures:** if a resume fails, you get a banner (`worker.armed_wake_continuation.failed`). Every skip and every save is logged as a named event.

**Verification**
- New tests replay this session's exact Monitor payload through the reaper and run a database round trip: save the wake, sweep, resume once, clear.
- The agent-runtime and continuation suites pass: 182 tests against a clean copy of HEAD plus my changes, and 34 more re-run in the live tree after a final test tweak.
- That clean copy also type-checks with no errors in the files I touched.
- The full `tests/server` run had 6 failures, none in this change:
  - `worker-models` and `supervisor-ordering-source` fail on another session's uncommitted edits to `worker-models.ts` and `conversations/sync.ts`.
  - `claude-model-pin` passes when run on its own.

Another session has uncommitted edits in `runtime-client.ts`, `named-events.ts` and `goal-worker-continuation.ts`. I committed only my lines in those files and left theirs unstaged and untouched.

**Not done:** the nightly restart doesn't wait for armed agents. With the resume in place, it now costs a wake-up message instead of hours.
